# QoS-LLM unified log – 2025-08-08T09:11:14
====================================================================================================
SYSTEM PROMPT
====================================================================================================
# Persona
You are a senior network performance analyst at a major Brazilian ISP. Your task is to diagnose Quality of Service (QoS) issues by interpreting measurement data from network probes.

# Experiment Context
You will analyze data from two parallel approaches, each defined by a different reference metric for detecting instability (statistical changes).

## Data Collection
- Probes: 16 Raspberry Pi devices ran Network Diagnostic Tool (NDT) tests every 30 minutes.
- Raw Metrics: Throughput (download/upload) and RTT (download/upload).
- Targets: RNP (Brazilian National Research and Education Network) servers.

## Methodological Pipeline
1. Change-Point Detection: For each client-server pair, statistical change-points were detected for the reference metric, slicing the timeline into "intervals" of homogeneous behavior.
2. Multivariate Interval Characterization: While each interval is defined by a single reference metric (either throughput or RTT), every interval was characterized by the local mean and standard deviation of ALL FOUR metrics.
3. Clustering and Logistic Regression:
    - A clustering algorithm grouped all intervals into exactly two clusters: Cluster 0 and Cluster 1.
    - The clustering algorithm uses ALL interval's features to define the clusters: time, event occurrence, client/server ID, and the local mean/std of all the four metrics.
    - The model uses multinomial logistic regression with Cluster 0 as the baseline to define the probability of an interval belonging to one cluster or the other.
    - CRUCIAL: The logistic regression coefficients you will see are for the log-odds of an interval belonging to Cluster 1. A positive coefficient for a variable increases the odds of being in Cluster 1; a negative coefficient increases the odds of being in Cluster 0.

# Your Five-Stage Mission
1. Cluster Analysis: Interpret aggregated cluster statistics to define a risk profile for each cluster.
2. Critical Element Identification: Use the logistic regression coefficients to identify clients and servers most strongly associated with the high risk cluster.
3. Interval Diagnosis: Analyze the temporal performance evolution of each ofthese critical elements.
4. Technical Report per Approach: Consolidate findings for each approach (throughput and RTT) into a technical report.
5. Consolidated Operational Report: Synthesize both technical reports into a single, actionable document for network operations teams.

# Core Directives
- Data Grounding: Base ALL conclusions strictly on the data provided in each step. Do not extrapolate or assume.
- Output Language: All technical analyses (Stages 1-4) must be in expert-level English. The final Consolidated Operational Report (Stage 5) MUST be written in clear, accessible Brazilian Portuguese (PT-BR), keeping technical terms like "RTT" and "throughput" in English.
- Formatting: Use Markdown `##` titles for structure. DO NOT write tables.

## Task

For each cluster (Cluster 0 and Cluster 1), provide the following analysis:

1. Risk Profile Assignment: Label the cluster as either "High-Risk Profile (Unstable)" or "Low-Risk Profile (Stable)".
2. Justification: Provide a concise rationale for your assignment based on three key areas:
      - Connection Stability: Analyze `time_days_mean`, `event_frequency`, and `survival_probability`. Shorter durations and lower survival probabilities indicate instability.
      - Throughput Performance: Evaluate the mean values for `throughput_download` and `throughput_upload`.
      - Latency Performance: Evaluate the mean values for `rtt_download` and `rtt_upload`.

Structure your response under a main Markdown heading "## Cluster Profile Analysis", with a separate subsection for each cluster.

## Cluster Profile Analysis

### Cluster 1
- Risk Profile: Low-Risk Profile (Stable)

- Justification:
  - Connection Stability:
    - Mean interval duration ≈ 24.29 days (long).
    - Event frequency = 0.078 (low).
    - Survival: 1.00 at 7 days, 0.989 at 15 days, 0.887 at 30 days, 0.792 at 60–90 days — high persistence of stable intervals.

    - Upload mean ≈ 684.29.
    - Both are high relative to Cluster 0.
  - Latency Performance:
    - RTT download mean ≈ 16.04 ms.
    - RTT upload mean ≈ 18.87 ms.
    - Low RTTs indicate good latency.

### Cluster 0
- Risk Profile: High-Risk Profile (Unstable)

- Justification:
  - Connection Stability:
    - Mean interval duration ≈ 10.64 days (short).
    - Event frequency = 0.738 (high).
    - Survival: 0.941 at 1 day, 0.553 at 7 days, 0.18 at 15–30 days, ~0.158 by 60–90 days — rapid decay, indicating frequent instability.
  - Throughput Performance:

    - Both lower than Cluster 1.
  - Latency Performance:
    - RTT download mean ≈ 59.59 ms.

    - Elevated RTTs signal degraded latency performance.

====================================================================================================
[THROUGHPUT_DOWNLOAD] STAGE 3 – RESPONSE
====================================================================================================
## Diagnosis: client02

- Temporal behavior
  - Intervals by cluster: 4/28 (14%) in Cluster 1 and 24/28 (86%) in Cluster 0.
  - Stability overview:
    - Cluster 0 (rnp_sp, rnp_rj): Generally stable throughput and RTT across May–Jul, with two short degradations:
      - rnp_rj (2024-06-02 to 2024-06-04): download throughput dipped to ~554 Mb/s with high variability (std ~78 Mb/s) and RTT_upload_mean spiked to ~25.38 ms.
      - rnp_sp (2024-06-06 to 2024-06-07): download throughput mean ~579.7 Mb/s with elevated variability (std ~37 Mb/s); recovered afterward.
    - Cluster 1 (gru03, gru05, gru06): Present throughout the observation window for these sites. Metrics are broadly similar to Cluster 0 except:
      - gru05 shows a long sub-interval (2024-05-11 to 2024-07-15) with markedly lower download mean (~557 Mb/s) and very high variability (std ~92.6 Mb/s), indicating sustained instability on this path.
      - RTTs to GRU sites are moderate (~11–13 ms), lower than rnp_rj (~16–19 ms) but higher than rnp_sp (~9–11 ms); uploads to GRU sites are slightly lower on average (~296.5 Mb/s) than to RNP sites (~299–301 Mb/s).

- Key interactions (high-risk association)
  - All Cluster 1 intervals involve GRU sites: gru03, gru05, gru06.
  - Among these, gru05 is the standout contributor to instability due to the prolonged high download variability (std ~92.6 Mb/s) and reduced mean throughput.

- Actionable insight
  - Hypothesis: The persistent placement of GRU paths—particularly gru05—into the high-risk cluster is driven by elevated download throughput variability on those paths, suggesting intermittent congestion or path instability specific to the GRU routing/peering segment.
  - Test: Run time-correlated traceroute/MTR and targeted NDT/iperf from client02 to gru05/gru03/gru06 and compare against rnp_sp as a control. Correlate with interface/queue metrics and any routing changes during 2024-05-11 to 2024-07-15 for the GRU paths to confirm whether congestion or routing asymmetry is causing the variability. Additionally, review the brief rnp_rj degradation window (2024-06-02 to 2024-06-04) for transient routing changes.

## Diagnosis: client07

1) Temporal behavior
- Overall clustering: 27 intervals total; 17 (≈63%) in Cluster 0 and 10 (≈37%) in Cluster 1. For this element, Cluster 0 aligns with degraded/volatile performance (lower download throughput and/or very high variance), while Cluster 1 aligns with high throughput and low RTT.
- Early stability, mid-June shift:
  - Stable/high performance (Cluster 1) through mid-June to rnp_rj and rnp_sp, and consistently to gru05 and gru06 across the whole period (throughput ≈760–880+ Mb/s, RTT ≈12–20 ms).
  - From 2024-06-12 (rnp_sp) and 2024-06-16 (rnp_rj) onward, multiple change-point intervals (event=1) move to Cluster 0 with marked download volatility: rnp_rj later drops to ≈376–380 Mb/s with very high std (≈304–405 Mb/s) between 2024-07-06 and 2024-07-22; rnp_sp drops sharply by 2024-07-06–2024-07-23 (mean ≈522 Mb/s; std ≈421 Mb/s). RTT for rnp_rj/rnp_sp remains low and stable (~18–20/13–15 ms), indicating throughput issues not driven by latency inflation.
- gru03 path:
  - 2024-05-02 to 2024-07-09: Cluster 0 with high RTT (≈120–212 ms) and modest download (≈95–166 Mb/s), including a brief severe RTT spike (≈207 ms) around 2024-06-28–2024-07-01.
  - 2024-07-09–2024-07-17: still Cluster 0 but transiently very high throughput (≈538/619 Mb/s) with low RTT (~20 ms) and high variance.
  - 2024-07-17–2024-07-23: Cluster 1 stabilization (RTT ≈14–20 ms; upload ≈619 Mb/s; download ≈170 Mb/s) with low latency and reduced variance.

2) Key interactions in high-risk (Cluster 0) intervals
- rnp_rj: 7 Cluster 0 intervals after 2024-06-16, including pronounced download degradation and volatility from 2024-07-06 onward.
- rnp_sp: 5 Cluster 0 intervals after 2024-06-12, culminating in strong download instability and reduction from 2024-07-06 onward.
- gru03: 5 Cluster 0 intervals from 2024-05-02 to 2024-07-17, characterized by abnormally high RTT until early July.
- Consistently healthy: gru05 and gru06 (all intervals in Cluster 1, high throughput, low RTT).

3) Actionable insight (testable hypothesis)
- Hypothesis: A configuration or capacity change around mid-June affected the downstream data plane for client07 specifically toward rnp_rj and rnp_sp, causing throughput instability and occasional reduction without RTT inflation—consistent with loss-based congestion or policing/shaping rather than path latency problems. 
- Test plan:
  - From client07, run sustained TCP tests (iperf/NDT) to rnp_rj and rnp_sp while capturing packet loss/retransmissions; correlate with interface counters (drops, discards) on the access/aggregation path.
  - Run mtr/traceroute during the degraded window to confirm stable latency but detect intermittent loss; review any policy/capacity changes introduced between 2024-06-12 and 2024-06-16.
  - Separately, review historical routing to gru03 for May–early July to explain the earlier high RTT (120–210 ms); confirm route normalization after 2024-07-09 with repeated traceroute and latency baselines.

## Task

Provide a concise diagnostic for `rnp_rj`:

1. Summarize Temporal Behavior: Describe the element's performance evolution. Was it consistently unstable, or were there specific periods of degradation? Compare the proportion of intervals in each cluster.
2. Identify Key Interactions: Note any partner servers or clients that consistently appear in its high-risk intervals.
3. Formulate an Actionable Insight: Conclude with a specific, testable hypothesis for the operations team (e.g., "The consistent high RTT during interactions with `rnp_rj` suggests a potential routing issue to be investigated via `traceroute`.").

Produce a subsection titled "Diagnosis: rnp_rj".



====================================================================================================
[THROUGHPUT_DOWNLOAD] STAGE 3 – RESPONSE
====================================================================================================
## Diagnosis: rnp_rj

- Temporal behavior
  - Across 76 intervals, 42 were in Cluster 1 (~55%) and 34 in Cluster 0 (~45%). Instability was not constant; specific degradation windows stand out.
  - Coordinated download degradations occurred mid-July 2024:
    - 2024-07-17 to 2024-07-23: sharp download drops with uploads broadly maintained and RTT roughly steady (18–24 ms):
      - client08 (182.6 Mbps, 07-19→07-23, Cluster 1)
      - client09 (219.2 Mbps, 07-21→07-23, Cluster 1)
      - client13 (248.6 Mbps, 07-17→07-22 Cluster 0, then 07-22→07-23 Cluster 1)
      - client14 (220.4 Mbps, 07-17→07-19, Cluster 1)
      - client16 (182.3 Mbps, 07-19→07-22, Cluster 0; partial recovery 07-22→07-23)
      - client07 (376–380 Mbps, 07-06→07-22, Cluster 0; recovery 07-22→07-23)
  - Persistent RTT increase for client04 starting 2024-06-26 (Cluster 1): RTT_download ≈17.4 ms and RTT_upload ≈30.4 ms versus prior ≈2–3 ms, with high throughput maintained—consistent with a path change rather than capacity loss.
  - Brief RTT spike for client01 in 2024-05-15→05-21 (Cluster 0): RTT_download ≈21.5 ms, then normalized.

- Key interactions (clients frequently in higher-risk situations)
  - Clients consistently in Cluster 1 throughout their observation windows: client03, client05, client06, client10, client11, client12. Note: despite Cluster 1 dominance, many of these show high throughput; cluster membership alone does not always imply degradation for these clients.
  - Clients exhibiting clear mid-July download collapses: client08, client09, client13, client14, client16 (and client07 with a longer low-download spell). These events are temporally aligned across independent clients.
  - Stable reference: client02 ran entirely in Cluster 0 with steady download ~580–591 Mbps and low variability—useful as a control path.

- Actionable insight (testable)
  - Hypothesis: A server-to-client path issue (e.g., congestion/queuing or peering anomaly on rnp_rj egress toward the ISP) occurred around 2024-07-17 to 2024-07-22, causing synchronized download degradation across multiple clients while uploads and RTT stayed broadly stable.
  - Tests:
    - Reproduce during similar hours with parallel NDT/iperf from affected clients (08, 09, 13, 14, 16, 07) and an unaffected control (client02).
    - Collect bidirectional traceroute/MTR during the test window; compare AS paths/hops between affected clients and client02.
    - Inspect rnp_rj egress interface counters, queue/CoDel/AQM stats, and peering/BGP change logs for 2024-07-17→07-22.
    - For client04, verify routing changes since 2024-06-26 (why RTT jumped from ~3 ms to ~17–30 ms despite high throughput).

## Task

Provide a concise diagnostic for `rnp_sp`:

1. Summarize Temporal Behavior: Describe the element's performance evolution. Was it consistently unstable, or were there specific periods of degradation? Compare the proportion of intervals in each cluster.
2. Identify Key Interactions: Note any partner servers or clients that consistently appear in its high-risk intervals.
3. Formulate an Actionable Insight: Conclude with a specific, testable hypothesis for the operations team (e.g., "The consistent high RTT during interactions with `rnp_rj` suggests a potential routing issue to be investigated via `traceroute`.").

Produce a subsection titled "Diagnosis: rnp_sp".

[THROUGHPUT_DOWNLOAD] STAGE 3 – RESPONSE

## Diagnosis: rnp_sp

- Temporal behavior and clusters
  - Intervals observed: 58 total across clients to rnp_sp.
  - Cluster distribution: 34/58 (≈58.6%) in Cluster 1, 24/58 (≈41.4%) in Cluster 0.
  - Degradation pattern is not constant; it concentrates in specific windows:
    - 2024-07-17 to 2024-07-23: multiple clients show a sharp, sustained drop in download throughput to ~175–232 Mbps while upload remains high (~790–830 Mbps). RTT stays near historical values (≈12–16 ms) for most clients, with the exception of client08 which sees RTT_download rise to ~26 ms with very high variability.
    - Early July precursors: client07 shows a pronounced drop/instability starting 2024-07-06 (download mean ~522 Mbps with very large variance), and client09 exhibits a transient instability 2024-07-04 to 2024-07-09 (large variance).
    - Prior to mid-July, most clients sustained high download performance (≈600–900+ Mbps) with RTT_download ≈9–16 ms.
  - Note: Degraded intervals span both clusters depending on client (e.g., clients 08/12/13 degraded intervals include Cluster 0 and 1), so the cluster label alone is not a reliable proxy for “bad” vs “good” on this site.

- Key interactions (clients repeatedly present in degraded windows)
  - 2024-07-17 onward (consistent low download ~175–232 Mbps, upload stable high):
    - client08 (Cluster 0): DL ≈175.7 Mbps, UL ≈828.8 Mbps; RTT_download rises to ≈26.4 ms with very high jitter.
    - client12 (Cluster 0 then Cluster 1): DL ≈232.6 → ≈219.0 Mbps, UL ≈708.6 Mbps; RTT_download near normal.
    - client13 (Cluster 0 then Cluster 1): DL ≈174 → ≈177 Mbps, UL ≈796.1 Mbps; RTT_download near normal.
    - client14 (Cluster 1): DL ≈176.5 Mbps, UL ≈794.1 Mbps; RTT_download near normal.
    - client16 (Cluster 1): DL ≈176.3 Mbps, UL ≈792.3 Mbps; RTT_download near normal.
  - Other notable degradations:
    - client07: 2024-07-06 to 2024-07-23 (Cluster 0): DL mean ≈522 Mbps with extremely high variance (std ≈421 Mbps), UL ≈829.5 Mbps; RTT_download stable.
    - client09: 2024-07-04 to 2024-07-09 (Cluster 0): DL unstable (mean ≈629 Mbps, std ≈387 Mbps); partial recovery thereafter with reduced but still elevated variance.
  - In contrast, some Cluster 0 intervals reflect improved or very high download (e.g., client04 2024-06-26 to 2024-07-01: DL ≈910.6 Mbps), reinforcing that cluster assignment is not a universal “risk” marker here.

- Actionable insight (testable hypothesis)
  - Hypothesis: Starting around 2024-07-17, rnp_sp’s download path exhibits a ceiling near ~200 Mbps across multiple, independent clients, while upload remains unaffected and RTT is mostly unchanged. This signature is consistent with a download-path policy/shaping or capacity bottleneck close to rnp_sp (server-side or immediate downstream). The outlier (client08) shows concurrent RTT/jitter inflation, suggesting additional path congestion for that specific client.
  - Tests to validate:
    - Verify rnp_sp server and adjacent interface counters/configs for new/changed rate limits, policers, queueing policies, or congestion from 2024-07-17 onward.
    - Run controlled parallel throughput tests (e.g., iperf/NDT) from rnp_sp to multiple affected clients (08, 12, 13, 14, 16) and to at least one unaffected/less-affected client as control, comparing download vs upload.
    - Perform time-correlated traceroute/MTR from affected clients to rnp_sp during the issue window to detect path changes (AS hop shifts, increased loss/latency).
    - Cross-check BGP/peering changes and link utilization near rnp_sp during 2024-07-17–07-23 for signs of re-routing or saturation.

### Cluster 0

  - Stability overview:
    - Cluster 0 (rnp_sp, rnp_rj): Generally stable throughput and RTT across May–Jul, with two short degradations:
      - rnp_rj (2024-06-02 to 2024-06-04): download throughput dipped to ~554 Mb/s with high variability (std ~78 Mb/s) and RTT_upload_mean spiked to ~25.38 ms.
      - rnp_sp (2024-06-06 to 2024-06-07): download throughput mean ~579.7 Mb/s with elevated variability (std ~37 Mb/s); recovered afterward.
    - Cluster 1 (gru03, gru05, gru06): Present throughout the observation window for these sites. Metrics are broadly similar to Cluster 0 except:
      - gru05 shows a long sub-interval (2024-05-11 to 2024-07-15) with markedly lower download mean (~557 Mb/s) and very high variability (std ~92.6 Mb/s), indicating sustained instability on this path.
      - RTTs to GRU sites are moderate (~11–13 ms), lower than rnp_rj (~16–19 ms) but higher than rnp_sp (~9–11 ms); uploads to GRU sites are slightly lower on average (~296.5 Mb/s) than to RNP sites (~299–301 Mb/s).

- Key interactions (high-risk association)
  - All Cluster 1 intervals involve GRU sites: gru03, gru05, gru06.
  - Among these, gru05 is the standout contributor to instability due to the prolonged high download variability (std ~92.6 Mb/s) and reduced mean throughput.

- Actionable insight
  - Hypothesis: The persistent placement of GRU paths—particularly gru05—into the high-risk cluster is driven by elevated download throughput variability on those paths, suggesting intermittent congestion or path instability specific to the GRU routing/peering segment.
  - Test: Run time-correlated traceroute/MTR and targeted NDT/iperf from client02 to gru05/gru03/gru06 and compare against rnp_sp as a control. Correlate with interface/queue metrics and any routing changes during 2024-05-11 to 2024-07-15 for the GRU paths to confirm whether congestion or routing asymmetry is causing the variability. Additionally, review the brief rnp_rj degradation window (2024-06-02 to 2024-06-04) for transient routing changes.

## Diagnosis: client07

1) Temporal behavior
- Overall clustering: 27 intervals total; 17 (≈63%) in Cluster 0 and 10 (≈37%) in Cluster 1. For this element, Cluster 0 aligns with degraded/volatile performance (lower download throughput and/or very high variance), while Cluster 1 aligns with high throughput and low RTT.
- Early stability, mid-June shift:
  - Stable/high performance (Cluster 1) through mid-June to rnp_rj and rnp_sp, and consistently to gru05 and gru06 across the whole period (throughput ≈760–880+ Mb/s, RTT ≈12–20 ms).
  - From 2024-06-12 (rnp_sp) and 2024-06-16 (rnp_rj) onward, multiple change-point intervals (event=1) move to Cluster 0 with marked download volatility: rnp_rj later drops to ≈376–380 Mb/s with very high std (≈304–405 Mb/s) between 2024-07-06 and 2024-07-22; rnp_sp drops sharply by 2024-07-06–2024-07-23 (mean ≈522 Mb/s; std ≈421 Mb/s). RTT for rnp_rj/rnp_sp remains low and stable (~18–20/13–15 ms), indicating throughput issues not driven by latency inflation.
- gru03 path:
  - 2024-05-02 to 2024-07-09: Cluster 0 with high RTT (≈120–212 ms) and modest download (≈95–166 Mb/s), including a brief severe RTT spike (≈207 ms) around 2024-06-28–2024-07-01.
  - 2024-07-09–2024-07-17: still Cluster 0 but transiently very high throughput (≈538/619 Mb/s) with low RTT (~20 ms) and high variance.
  - 2024-07-17–2024-07-23: Cluster 1 stabilization (RTT ≈14–20 ms; upload ≈619 Mb/s; download ≈170 Mb/s) with low latency and reduced variance.

2) Key interactions in high-risk (Cluster 0) intervals
- rnp_rj: 7 Cluster 0 intervals after 2024-06-16, including pronounced download degradation and volatility from 2024-07-06 onward.
- rnp_sp: 5 Cluster 0 intervals after 2024-06-12, culminating in strong download instability and reduction from 2024-07-06 onward.
- gru03: 5 Cluster 0 intervals from 2024-05-02 to 2024-07-17, characterized by abnormally high RTT until early July.
- Consistently healthy: gru05 and gru06 (all intervals in Cluster 1, high throughput, low RTT).

3) Actionable insight (testable hypothesis)
- Hypothesis: A configuration or capacity change around mid-June affected the downstream data plane for client07 specifically toward rnp_rj and rnp_sp, causing throughput instability and occasional reduction without RTT inflation—consistent with loss-based congestion or policing/shaping rather than path latency problems. 
- Test plan:
  - From client07, run sustained TCP tests (iperf/NDT) to rnp_rj and rnp_sp while capturing packet loss/retransmissions; correlate with interface counters (drops, discards) on the access/aggregation path.
  - Run mtr/traceroute during the degraded window to confirm stable latency but detect intermittent loss; review any policy/capacity changes introduced between 2024-06-12 and 2024-06-16.
  - Separately, review historical routing to gru03 for May–early July to explain the earlier high RTT (120–210 ms); confirm route normalization after 2024-07-09 with repeated traceroute and latency baselines.

## Diagnosis: rnp_rj

- Temporal behavior
  - Across 76 intervals, 42 were in Cluster 1 (~55%) and 34 in Cluster 0 (~45%). Instability was not constant; specific degradation windows stand out.
  - Coordinated download degradations occurred mid-July 2024:
    - 2024-07-17 to 2024-07-23: sharp download drops with uploads broadly maintained and RTT roughly steady (18–24 ms):
      - client08 (182.6 Mbps, 07-19→07-23, Cluster 1)
      - client09 (219.2 Mbps, 07-21→07-23, Cluster 1)
      - client13 (248.6 Mbps, 07-17→07-22 Cluster 0, then 07-22→07-23 Cluster 1)
      - client14 (220.4 Mbps, 07-17→07-19, Cluster 1)
      - client16 (182.3 Mbps, 07-19→07-22, Cluster 0; partial recovery 07-22→07-23)
      - client07 (376–380 Mbps, 07-06→07-22, Cluster 0; recovery 07-22→07-23)
  - Persistent RTT increase for client04 starting 2024-06-26 (Cluster 1): RTT_download ≈17.4 ms and RTT_upload ≈30.4 ms versus prior ≈2–3 ms, with high throughput maintained—consistent with a path change rather than capacity loss.
  - Brief RTT spike for client01 in 2024-05-15→05-21 (Cluster 0): RTT_download ≈21.5 ms, then normalized.

- Key interactions (clients frequently in higher-risk situations)
  - Clients consistently in Cluster 1 throughout their observation windows: client03, client05, client06, client10, client11, client12. Note: despite Cluster 1 dominance, many of these show high throughput; cluster membership alone does not always imply degradation for these clients.
  - Clients exhibiting clear mid-July download collapses: client08, client09, client13, client14, client16 (and client07 with a longer low-download spell). These events are temporally aligned across independent clients.
  - Stable reference: client02 ran entirely in Cluster 0 with steady download ~580–591 Mbps and low variability—useful as a control path.

- Actionable insight (testable)
  - Hypothesis: A server-to-client path issue (e.g., congestion/queuing or peering anomaly on rnp_rj egress toward the ISP) occurred around 2024-07-17 to 2024-07-22, causing synchronized download degradation across multiple clients while uploads and RTT stayed broadly stable.
  - Tests:
    - Reproduce during similar hours with parallel NDT/iperf from affected clients (08, 09, 13, 14, 16, 07) and an unaffected control (client02).
    - Collect bidirectional traceroute/MTR during the test window; compare AS paths/hops between affected clients and client02.
    - Inspect rnp_rj egress interface counters, queue/CoDel/AQM stats, and peering/BGP change logs for 2024-07-17→07-22.
    - For client04, verify routing changes since 2024-06-26 (why RTT jumped from ~3 ms to ~17–30 ms despite high throughput).

## Diagnosis: rnp_sp

- Temporal behavior and clusters
  - Intervals observed: 58 total across clients to rnp_sp.
  - Cluster distribution: 34/58 (≈58.6%) in Cluster 1, 24/58 (≈41.4%) in Cluster 0.
  - Degradation pattern is not constant; it concentrates in specific windows:
    - 2024-07-17 to 2024-07-23: multiple clients show a sharp, sustained drop in download throughput to ~175–232 Mbps while upload remains high (~790–830 Mbps). RTT stays near historical values (≈12–16 ms) for most clients, with the exception of client08 which sees RTT_download rise to ~26 ms with very high variability.
    - Early July precursors: client07 shows a pronounced drop/instability starting 2024-07-06 (download mean ~522 Mbps with very large variance), and client09 exhibits a transient instability 2024-07-04 to 2024-07-09 (large variance).
    - Prior to mid-July, most clients sustained high download performance (≈600–900+ Mbps) with RTT_download ≈9–16 ms.
  - Note: Degraded intervals span both clusters depending on client (e.g., clients 08/12/13 degraded intervals include Cluster 0 and 1), so the cluster label alone is not a reliable proxy for “bad” vs “good” on this site.

- Key interactions (clients repeatedly present in degraded windows)
  - 2024-07-17 onward (consistent low download ~175–232 Mbps, upload stable high):
    - client08 (Cluster 0): DL ≈175.7 Mbps, UL ≈828.8 Mbps; RTT_download rises to ≈26.4 ms with very high jitter.
    - client12 (Cluster 0 then Cluster 1): DL ≈232.6 → ≈219.0 Mbps, UL ≈708.6 Mbps; RTT_download near normal.
    - client13 (Cluster 0 then Cluster 1): DL ≈174 → ≈177 Mbps, UL ≈796.1 Mbps; RTT_download near normal.
    - client14 (Cluster 1): DL ≈176.5 Mbps, UL ≈794.1 Mbps; RTT_download near normal.
    - client16 (Cluster 1): DL ≈176.3 Mbps, UL ≈792.3 Mbps; RTT_download near normal.
  - Other notable degradations:
    - client07: 2024-07-06 to 2024-07-23 (Cluster 0): DL mean ≈522 Mbps with extremely high variance (std ≈421 Mbps), UL ≈829.5 Mbps; RTT_download stable.
    - client09: 2024-07-04 to 2024-07-09 (Cluster 0): DL unstable (mean ≈629 Mbps, std ≈387 Mbps); partial recovery thereafter with reduced but still elevated variance.
  - In contrast, some Cluster 0 intervals reflect improved or very high download (e.g., client04 2024-06-26 to 2024-07-01: DL ≈910.6 Mbps), reinforcing that cluster assignment is not a universal “risk” marker here.

- Actionable insight (testable hypothesis)
  - Hypothesis: Starting around 2024-07-17, rnp_sp’s download path exhibits a ceiling near ~200 Mbps across multiple, independent clients, while upload remains unaffected and RTT is mostly unchanged. This signature is consistent with a download-path policy/shaping or capacity bottleneck close to rnp_sp (server-side or immediate downstream). The outlier (client08) shows concurrent RTT/jitter inflation, suggesting additional path congestion for that specific client.
  - Tests to validate:
    - Verify rnp_sp server and adjacent interface counters/configs for new/changed rate limits, policers, queueing policies, or congestion from 2024-07-17 onward.
    - Run controlled parallel throughput tests (e.g., iperf/NDT) from rnp_sp to multiple affected clients (08, 12, 13, 14, 16) and to at least one unaffected/less-affected client as control, comparing download vs upload.
    - Perform time-correlated traceroute/MTR from affected clients to rnp_sp during the issue window to detect path changes (AS hop shifts, increased loss/latency).
    - Cross-check BGP/peering changes and link utilization near rnp_sp during 2024-07-17–07-23 for signs of re-routing or saturation.

## QoS Assessment Report: throughput_download Approach

### 1) Executive Summary
Two distinct performance clusters emerged. Cluster 1 represents a low-risk, stable profile (longer intervals, low event frequency, higher download/upload throughput, and low RTT). Cluster 0 represents a high-risk, unstable profile (shorter intervals, high event frequency, lower throughput, and elevated RTT). Logistic regression associates client02, client07, rnp_rj, and rnp_sp with higher odds of Cluster 0. Time-series diagnoses show: (a) client07 shifted to unstable download performance from mid-June toward rnp_rj and rnp_sp without RTT inflation, and had earlier high-RTT issues to gru03 that later normalized; (b) client02 is predominantly Cluster 0, with notable download variability toward gru05; (c) rnp_rj and rnp_sp show coordinated mid-July download collapses across multiple clients while uploads and RTT largely remained stable, consistent with downstream capacity/policy effects near the servers or adjacent peering. Cluster labels align with aggregated risk, but are not a perfect proxy per client/site, so interval metrics and timing remain essential.

### 2) Detailed Findings

- Cluster risk profiles (aggregated)
  - Cluster 1 (low-risk, stable): Long intervals (≈24.29 days), very low event frequency (0.078), strong survival (≥0.887 at 30 days), higher throughput (DL ≈632.5, UL ≈684.3), and low RTT (DL ≈16.0 ms, UL ≈18.9 ms).
  - Cluster 0 (high-risk, unstable): Short intervals (≈10.64 days), high event frequency (0.738), fast decay (survival 0.553 at 7 days; ~0.18 at 15–30 days), lower throughput (DL ≈452.1, UL ≈429.3), and elevated RTT (DL ≈59.6 ms, UL ≈60.6 ms).

- Critical elements (logistic regression; higher odds of Cluster 0)
  - Clients: client02 (coef −1.95), client07 (coef −1.73).
  - Servers: rnp_rj (coef −1.37), rnp_sp (coef −1.23).

- Interval diagnoses

  client02
  - Cluster distribution: 24/28 intervals (86%) in Cluster 0; 4/28 (14%) in Cluster 1.
  - RNP paths (rnp_sp, rnp_rj): Mostly steady behavior across May–Jul with two short degradations:
    - rnp_rj: 2024-06-02 to 2024-06-04, DL dipped to ~554 Mbps with high variability (std ~78 Mbps); RTT_upload_mean ~25.38 ms.
    - rnp_sp: 2024-06-06 to 2024-06-07, DL ~579.7 Mbps with elevated variability (std ~37 Mbps); recovered afterward.
  - GRU paths (gru03, gru05, gru06): Present throughout; gru05 shows a long sub-interval 2024-05-11 to 2024-07-15 with lower DL (~557 Mbps) and very high variability (std ~92.6 Mbps), indicating sustained instability on that path.
  - Note: Although Cluster 1 is generally stable in aggregate, for client02 the GRU-assigned intervals include the most pronounced download variability (gru05), underscoring that cluster labels are not a perfect per-path proxy.

  client07
  - Cluster distribution: 17/27 (≈63%) in Cluster 0; 10/27 (≈37%) in Cluster 1.
  - Mid-June shift to instability toward RNP servers:
    - rnp_sp: from 2024-06-12, multiple change-point intervals move to Cluster 0; by 2024-07-06–07-23 DL mean ≈522 Mbps with extremely high variance (std ≈421 Mbps); RTT stable (~13–15 ms).
    - rnp_rj: from 2024-06-16, Cluster 0 intervals accumulate; 2024-07-06–07-22 DL ≈376–380 Mbps with very high std (≈304–405 Mbps); RTT ~18–20 ms and stable.
  - gru03 path: 2024-05-02 to 2024-07-09 had abnormally high RTT (~120–212 ms) with modest DL; 2024-07-09–07-17 transient high DL/low RTT but high variance; after 2024-07-17, stabilized with low RTT (~14–20 ms).
  - Consistently healthy to GRU: gru05 and gru06 stayed in Cluster 1 with high throughput and low RTT.

  rnp_rj
  - Cluster mix: 42/76 (≈55%) in Cluster 1; 34/76 (≈45%) in Cluster 0.
  - Coordinated mid-July download degradations (uploads and RTT roughly steady at ~18–24 ms):
    - 2024-07-17→07-23 affecting multiple clients: client08 (DL ~182.6 Mbps), client09 (~219.2 Mbps), client13 (~248.6 Mbps), client14 (~220.4 Mbps), client16 (~182.3 Mbps), and client07 (longer low-DL spell ~376–380 Mbps, 07-06→07-22).
  - Notable routing/latency change: client04 from 2024-06-26 shows RTT_upload ≈30.4 ms (up from ~2–3 ms) while maintaining high throughput—consistent with a path change rather than capacity loss.

  rnp_sp
  - Cluster mix: 34/58 (≈58.6%) in Cluster 1; 24/58 (≈41.4%) in Cluster 0.
  - Mid-July download ceiling across multiple clients with stable upload:
    - 2024-07-17→07-23: client08 (DL ~175.7 Mbps; RTT_download ~26.4 ms with high jitter), client12 (~232.6→~219.0 Mbps), client13 (~174→~177 Mbps), client14 (~176.5 Mbps), client16 (~176.3 Mbps). Upload sustained high (~790–830 Mbps); RTT mostly near historical values except client08.
  - Precursors:
    - client07: 2024-07-06→07-23, DL mean ≈522 Mbps with very high variance (std ≈421 Mbps), RTT stable.
    - client09: 2024-07-04→07-09, DL instability (mean ≈629 Mbps, std ≈387 Mbps); partial recovery with lingering variance.
  - Cluster label caveat: Some Cluster 0 intervals reflect high performance (e.g., client04 ~910.6 Mbps DL on 2024-06-26→07-01), so label ≠ universal “bad.”

### 3) Recommended Actions

- Correlate and reproduce mid-July degradations
  - Run parallel NDT/iperf tests during comparable hours from affected clients (07, 08, 09, 12, 13, 14, 16) to rnp_rj and rnp_sp, plus an unaffected control path (e.g., client02→RNP).
  - Capture TCP retransmissions/loss, cwnd evolution, and per-flow goodput; compare download vs upload asymmetries.

- Path and routing verification
  - Perform bidirectional traceroute/MTR during degradation windows; record AS paths/hop stability and per-hop loss/latency.
  - Review BGP/peering change logs and link utilizations around 2024-07-17→07-23 (rnp_rj and rnp_sp) and 2024-06-12→06-16 (client07 onset).

- Server-side and adjacent interface checks (rnp_rj, rnp_sp)
  - Inspect egress/ingress interface counters (drops, discards), queue/AQM stats, shaping/policing policies, and any recent config changes.
  - Specifically validate absence of download-path rate limits near ~200 Mbps on/near rnp_sp in 2024-07-17→07-23.

- Client-specific investigations
  - client07: Audit access/aggregation device counters for drops/oversubscription; verify no new policers/shapers introduced mid-June. Repeat sustained tests to rnp_rj/rnp_sp and confirm stable RTT with signs of loss-based throughput collapse.
  - client02: Target gru05/gru03/gru06 vs rnp_sp as control; correlate high download variability (2024-05-11→07-15 to gru05) with path/routing/interface data; check for intermittent congestion on GRU segments.

- Historic anomalies
  - Validate routing changes for client04→rnp_rj around 2024-06-26 that raised RTT without throughput loss.
  - For client07→gru03, confirm route normalization post 2024-07-09 with baseline RTT monitoring.

- Monitoring and alerting
  - Implement detectors for synchronized multi-client download depressions and for “download ceiling” patterns (e.g., ~200 Mbps) with unaffected upload/RTT.
  - Track cluster event rates per client/server to catch rising instability before customer impact.

[RTT_UPLOAD] STAGE 3 – RESPONSE
====================================================================================================
## Diagnosis: client09

1) Temporal behavior
- Two distinct regimes split by target site:
  - Cluster 1 (low-risk): 8/16 intervals. Targets: gru05, gru06, rnp_rj, rnp_sp. Performance consistently excellent — throughput download 831–905 Mbps, upload 859–871 Mbps; RTT 12–20 ms with low variability.
  - Cluster 0 (high-risk for this client): 8/16 intervals, exclusively to site gru03. Typical state (most of May–June): throughput download ~170 Mbps, upload ~182 Mbps, RTT ~116–121 ms, occasional elevated variability.
- Specific degradations to gru03:
  - 2024-06-28 → 2024-07-01: severe impairment — download 93.9 Mbps, upload 100.4 Mbps, RTT ~212 ms, high variance across all metrics.
  - 2024-07-09 → 2024-07-19: asymmetric improvement — RTT drops to ~15 ms and upload jumps to ~867 Mbps, but download remains ~172 Mbps.
  - 2024-07-19 → 2024-07-23: RTT stays low (~13–15 ms) and upload remains high (~866 Mbps), but download becomes unstable (mean ~147 Mbps; very high std ~91 Mbps).
- Change signals: 6/8 Cluster 0 intervals are flagged as events, while all Cluster 1 intervals have no event flags — consistent with instability concentrated at gru03.

2) Key interactions
- High-risk intervals are exclusively associated with the server/site gru03.
- All other partners (gru05, gru06, rnp_rj, rnp_sp) remain in the low-risk cluster with stable, high throughput and low RTT.

3) Actionable insight (testable hypothesis)
- Hypothesis: Issues are specific to the path and/or server-side at gru03. Before 2024-07-09, a suboptimal route to gru03 elevated RTT (~120 ms). After 2024-07-09, routing improved (RTT ~15 ms), but download-only instability persisted, suggesting download-plane congestion or rate limiting at gru03 or along the server-to-client path.
- Tests:
  - Run traceroute/MTR from client09 to gru03 and reverse (from gru03 to client09) to validate current routing and latency; compare with historical paths if available.
  - Perform directional iperf3 to gru03 and, in parallel, to a healthy reference (e.g., gru06) to confirm download-only degradation is localized to gru03.
  - Check gru03 telemetry (CPU/NIC utilization, queueing, TCP retransmissions) and any BGP/peering changes around 2024-06-28 → 07-01 and post-07-09.
  - If possible, capture server-to-client flow metrics (AQM/queue drops) to verify download-path congestion specific to gru03.

## Diagnosis: rnp_rj

1) Temporal behavior
- Cluster distribution for rnp_rj: 44 total intervals, with 33 in Cluster 1 (~75%) and 11 in Cluster 0 (~25%). Cluster 0 intervals are consistently flagged as change events (event=1), while Cluster 1 is predominantly event=0.
- Interpreted risk profile (from the interval metrics): Cluster 0 captures degradation/instability (higher RTT and/or higher variance, occasional throughput dips). Cluster 1 captures stable/baseline behavior.
- Client-specific evolution:
  - client01:
    - Stable baseline (Cluster 1) with very low RTT (rtt_d ~7.3 ms, rtt_u ~10–11 ms): 2024-05-01–05-07, 2024-06-01–07-11, 2024-07-18–07-23.
    - Degradation windows (Cluster 0, all event=1):
      - 2024-05-07 22:59–2024-06-01 14:30 (~24.6 days): rtt_d rose to ~18.5–21.5 ms (from ~7 ms), rtt_u ~21.2→9.8 ms (variable).
      - 2024-07-11 19:18–2024-07-18 12:48 (~6.7 days): fluctuating RTT (rtt_d ~8→15.5 ms; rtt_u ~9.4→15.0 ms) and notable throughput volatility with a download mean drop to ~495 Mbps (std ~72 Mbps).
  - client02:
    - Stable baseline (Cluster 1): 2024-05-01–05-08 and 2024-06-15–07-23.
    - Prolonged instability (Cluster 0, all event=1): 2024-05-08 20:50–2024-06-15 03:29 (~37.3 days) marked by uplink RTT elevation/variance (rtt_u up to ~25–26 ms, std up to ~17 ms) and download throughput dip/variance (down to ~554 Mbps, std ~78 Mbps).
- Other clients (03–16) remained entirely in Cluster 1 across their timelines, indicating the server’s overall service to most probes was stable during the study window.

2) Key interactions (high-risk cluster presence)
- Only client01 and client02 generated Cluster 0 intervals with rnp_rj.
- No other clients appear in the high-risk cluster for rnp_rj, reinforcing that observed degradations are path-specific to these two client–server pairs rather than server-wide.

3) Actionable insight
- Testable hypothesis: The degradations are path-specific between client01/client02 and rnp_rj, characterized by elevated RTT and throughput variability during 2024-05-07→06-01 (client01), 2024-07-11→07-18 (client01), and 2024-05-08→06-15 (client02). Investigate routing/congestion on these paths.
- Actions:
  - Run time-correlated traceroute/MTR from client01 and client02 to rnp_rj (and reverse) during similar dayparts to those windows to detect path changes or hop-specific latency.
  - Check BGP/IGP change logs and interface counters (errors/drops/queueing) on the rnp_rj ingress/egress facing client01/client02 networks around the cited dates.
  - If available, compare queue/traffic shaping policies applied to client01/client02 paths versus other clients that remained stable.

## Diagnosis: client12

1) Temporal behavior
- Cluster profile: Intervals labeled as Cluster 1 show high throughput (~680–750 Mbps) and low RTT (~12–21 ms), while Cluster 0 aligns with degraded conditions (higher RTT and/or lower/unstable throughput). client12 spent 9/17 intervals in Cluster 1 and 8/17 in Cluster 0.
- By site:
  - gru03: Persistent degradation from 2024-05-06 to 2024-07-09 (Cluster 0). Early period had RTT ~120–123 ms at ~164/174 Mbps; 2024-06-28 to 2024-07-01 worsened sharply (RTT ~223–229 ms, download ~81 Mbps). 2024-07-01 to 2024-07-09 remained unstable (RTT ~75–105 ms, very high variability). After 2024-07-09, performance flipped to Cluster 1 with strong throughput (~670–748 Mbps) and low RTT (~14–19 ms) through 2024-07-23.
  - gru05: Stable and good until 2024-07-08 (Cluster 1). Brief degradation 2024-07-08 to 2024-07-14 (Cluster 0) driven by higher and volatile download RTT (~26.7 ms, high std), throughput largely unchanged.
  - rnp_sp: Good until 2024-07-07 (Cluster 1). Short degradation 2024-07-07 to 2024-07-17 (Cluster 0) with elevated upload RTT (~14.33 ms vs ~11.68 prior), then recovery to Cluster 1 on 2024-07-17.
  - rnp_rj and gru06: Consistently good across the entire window (Cluster 1) with high throughput (~700–745 Mbps) and low RTT (~12–21 ms).

2) Key interactions (high-risk)
- Concentrated with gru03 (multiple, long Cluster 0 intervals with severe RTT inflation and throughput collapse prior to 2024-07-09).
- Transient, milder issues with gru05 (2024-07-08 to 2024-07-14) and rnp_sp (2024-07-07 to 2024-07-17).
- No high-risk intervals with rnp_rj or gru06.

3) Actionable insight
- Testable hypothesis: A path-specific issue to gru03 affected client12 until 2024-07-09 (elevated RTT up to ~229 ms and download collapse to ~81 Mbps), after which routing/capacity conditions changed and performance normalized. Validate by:
  - Running concurrent traceroute/MTR from client12 (and a control probe) to gru03, and reviewing historical path/BGP changes around 2024-06-28 to 2024-07-09.
  - Checking gru03 uplink utilization/queueing and any configuration changes in that window.
  - As a secondary check, audit routing events around 2024-07-07–07-14 for brief RTT rises observed to gru05 and rnp_sp.

## Diagnosis: client02

1) Temporal behavior
- Overall pattern: Mostly stable with brief, well-delimited degradations. Intervals by cluster: Cluster 1 = 7/12 (58%), Cluster 0 = 5/12 (42%).
- Stable periods (Cluster 1, event=0): Persistent across all three gru sites (gru03, gru05, gru06) and most of the time for rnp_sp and rnp_rj. Typical metrics: throughput download ≈ 582–593 Mb/s, upload ≈ 296–301 Mb/s; RTT download ≈ 9–12 ms (SP/GRU), ≈ 16–17 ms (RJ); low variability.
- Degradation windows (Cluster 0, event=1), concentrated with RNP:
  - rnp_sp: 2024-05-06 → 2024-05-08. RTT temporarily increased (rtt_download_mean 11.07 ms vs ~9–10 ms elsewhere) with higher RTT variability; reverted to stable after 2024-05-08.
  - rnp_rj: 2024-05-08 → 2024-06-15, split into multiple Cluster 0 intervals. Notable worsening on 2024-06-02 → 2024-06-04: download throughput dropped to ~554 Mb/s with very high variance (std ~78 Mb/s) and upload RTT spiked to ~25.4 ms with large variance. Post-6/4 remained in Cluster 0 with sustained lower download throughput (~554 Mb/s) until 2024-06-15, then recovered to Cluster 1. After recovery, upload RTT to rnp_rj stayed moderately higher (~19.7 ms) than early-May levels (~17.5–18 ms).

2) Key interactions
- High-risk intervals (Cluster 0) occur only with RNP servers:
  - rnp_rj: Multiple contiguous Cluster 0 intervals, including the worst episode (2024-06-02 → 2024-06-04) and a prolonged lower-throughput phase until 2024-06-15.
  - rnp_sp: One short Cluster 0 episode (2024-05-06 → 2024-05-08) with modest RTT increase.
- No high-risk intervals with gru03, gru05, or gru06 (all Cluster 1).

3) Actionable insight
- Hypothesis: The degradations are path/server-specific to RNP, particularly rnp_rj, with a transient severe event around 2024-06-02 → 2024-06-04 and lingering impact until 2024-06-15. This points to a routing/congestion or server-side issue on the RJ path.
- Next steps to test:
  - Run traceroute/MTR from client02 to rnp_rj and rnp_sp during busy and idle hours to compare hop-level RTT and loss.
  - Check historical routing/peering changes and interface utilization for the RJ path between 2024-05-08 and 2024-06-15 (focus on 2024-06-02 → 2024-06-04).

## Diagnosis: client07

Notable severe degradation from 2024-06-28 to 2024-07-01 (throughput ≈95/104 Mbps, RTT ≈207/212 ms), followed by partial recovery to ≈163/181 Mbps and RTT ≈120/122 ms. From 2024-07-09 to 2024-07-23, means improved (throughput ≈538/619 Mbps, RTT ≈20/20 ms) but with very large variability (stds high), still classified as Cluster 0, suggesting instability rather than consistently good performance.
  - New instability onset toward rnp_sp: Stable Cluster 1 performance until 2024-07-16, then Cluster 0 from 2024-07-16 to 2024-07-23 with highly variable downstream throughput (mean ≈522 Mbps, std ≈421 Mbps), while RTT remained low. Indicates recent onset of throughput instability specifically on this path.
  - Other paths (gru05, gru06, rnp_rj) remained consistently in Cluster 1 with high throughput (≈760–860 Mbps down, ≈550–790 Mbps up) and low RTT (≈12–20 ms) and low variability.

- Key interactions linked to high-risk intervals (Cluster 0)
  - Consistently high-risk: gru03 (all five intervals).
  - Recently high-risk: rnp_sp (only the last interval, 2024-07-16 to 2024-07-23).
  - Not high-risk: gru05, gru06, rnp_rj (all intervals in Cluster 1).

- Actionable insight
  - Hypothesis A (gru03): The persistently elevated RTT (≈120–212 ms) and lower/unstable throughput toward gru03, contrasted with good performance to all other sites, points to a path-specific impairment to gru03 (routing suboptimality or localized congestion). Test: run traceroute/MTR in both directions between client07 and gru03 during peak and off-peak; check BGP path/history and interface counters/queueing on interconnects to gru03 over the full window.
  - Hypothesis B (rnp_sp, starting 2024-07-16): A recent routing or server-side change introduced high downstream throughput variability while RTT stayed low. Test: correlate with change logs around 2024-07-16 for rnp_sp; compare traceroute paths pre/post 2024-07-16; perform controlled NDT bursts and packet-loss measurements (e.g., OWAMP) to detect microbursts/packet loss on the downstream path.

## ## Cluster Profile Analysis

### Cluster 0
- Risk Profile: High-Risk Profile (Unstable)
- Justification:
  - Connection Stability: Short intervals (time_mean ≈ 10.55, median ≈ 4.39), very high event_frequency (0.645), and rapid decay in survival_probability (0.91 at day 1 → 0.59 at day 7 → 0.266 at day 30 → 0.11 at day 60/90) indicate frequent changes and poor stability.
  - Throughput Performance: Lower means (download ≈ 306.6, upload ≈ 281.0) with large variability; underperforms relative to Cluster 1.
  - Latency Performance: High RTTs (download ≈ 96.35, upload ≈ 98.06), signaling significantly worse latency conditions.

### Cluster 1
- Risk Profile: Low-Risk Profile (Stable)
- Justification:
  - Connection Stability: Long intervals (time_mean ≈ 31.45, median ≈ 28.98), very low event_frequency (0.030), and strong survival_probability retention (1.0 through day 7, 0.961 at day 30, 0.933 at day 60/90) demonstrate sustained stability.
  - Throughput Performance: Higher means (download ≈ 660.7, upload ≈ 660.0) and stronger central tendency; substantially better than Cluster 0.
  - Latency Performance: Low RTTs (download ≈ 17.95, upload ≈ 20.41), consistent with healthy latency conditions.

## Critical Element Identification

  This large negative coefficient indicates a very strong tendency for this client’s intervals to fall into the unstable, high-risk Cluster 0.

- `rnp_rj` — Coefficient: -0.903056361978667

## Diagnosis: client09

1) Temporal behavior
- Two distinct regimes split by target site:
  - Cluster 1 (low-risk): 8/16 intervals. Targets: gru05, gru06, rnp_rj, rnp_sp. Performance consistently excellent — throughput download 831–905 Mbps, upload 859–871 Mbps; RTT 12–20 ms with low variability.
  - Cluster 0 (high-risk for this client): 8/16 intervals, exclusively to site gru03. Typical state (most of May–June): throughput download ~170 Mbps, upload ~182 Mbps, RTT ~116–121 ms, occasional elevated variability.
- Specific degradations to gru03:
  - 2024-06-28 → 2024-07-01: severe impairment — download 93.9 Mbps, upload 100.4 Mbps, RTT ~212 ms, high variance across all metrics.
  - 2024-07-09 → 2024-07-19: asymmetric improvement — RTT drops to ~15 ms and upload jumps to ~867 Mbps, but download remains ~172 Mbps.
  - 2024-07-19 → 2024-07-23: RTT stays low (~13–15 ms) and upload remains high (~866 Mbps), but download becomes unstable (mean ~147 Mbps; very high std ~91 Mbps).
- Change signals: 6/8 Cluster 0 intervals are flagged as events, while all Cluster 1 intervals have no event flags — consistent with instability concentrated at gru03.

2) Key interactions
- High-risk intervals are exclusively associated with the server/site gru03.
- All other partners (gru05, gru06, rnp_rj, rnp_sp) remain in the low-risk cluster with stable, high throughput and low RTT.

3) Actionable insight (testable hypothesis)
- Hypothesis: Issues are specific to the path and/or server-side at gru03. Before 2024-07-09, a suboptimal route to gru03 elevated RTT (~120 ms). After 2024-07-09, routing improved (RTT ~15 ms), but download-only instability persisted, suggesting download-plane congestion or rate limiting at gru03 or along the server-to-client path.
- Tests:
  - Run traceroute/MTR from client09 to gru03 and reverse (from gru03 to client09) to validate current routing and latency; compare with historical paths if available.
  - Perform directional iperf3 to gru03 and, in parallel, to a healthy reference (e.g., gru06) to confirm download-only degradation is localized to gru03.
  - Check gru03 telemetry (CPU/NIC utilization, queueing, TCP retransmissions) and any BGP/peering changes around 2024-06-28 → 07-01 and post-07-09.
  - If possible, capture server-to-client flow metrics (AQM/queue drops) to verify download-path congestion specific to gru03.

## Diagnosis: rnp_rj

1) Temporal behavior
- Cluster distribution for rnp_rj: 44 total intervals, with 33 in Cluster 1 (~75%) and 11 in Cluster 0 (~25%). Cluster 0 intervals are consistently flagged as change events (event=1), while Cluster 1 is predominantly event=0.
- Interpreted risk profile (from the interval metrics): Cluster 0 captures degradation/instability (higher RTT and/or higher variance, occasional throughput dips). Cluster 1 captures stable/baseline behavior.
- Client-specific evolution:
  - client01:
    - Stable baseline (Cluster 1) with very low RTT (rtt_d ~7.3 ms, rtt_u ~10–11 ms): 2024-05-01–05-07, 2024-06-01–07-11, 2024-07-18–07-23.
    - Degradation windows (Cluster 0, all event=1):
      - 2024-05-07 22:59–2024-06-01 14:30 (~24.6 days): rtt_d rose to ~18.5–21.5 ms (from ~7 ms), rtt_u ~21.2→9.8 ms (variable).
      - 2024-07-11 19:18–2024-07-18 12:48 (~6.7 days): fluctuating RTT (rtt_d ~8→15.5 ms; rtt_u ~9.4→15.0 ms) and notable throughput volatility with a download mean drop to ~495 Mbps (std ~72 Mbps).
  - client02:
    - Stable baseline (Cluster 1): 2024-05-01–05-08 and 2024-06-15–07-23.
    - Prolonged instability (Cluster 0, all event=1): 2024-05-08 20:50–2024-06-15 03:29 (~37.3 days) marked by uplink RTT elevation/variance (rtt_u up to ~25–26 ms, std up to ~17 ms) and download throughput dip/variance (down to ~554 Mbps, std ~78 Mbps).
- Other clients (03–16) remained entirely in Cluster 1 across their timelines, indicating the server’s overall service to most probes was stable during the study window.

2) Key interactions (high-risk cluster presence)
- Only client01 and client02 generated Cluster 0 intervals with rnp_rj.
- No other clients appear in the high-risk cluster for rnp_rj, reinforcing that observed degradations are path-specific to these two client–server pairs rather than server-wide.

3) Actionable insight
- Testable hypothesis: The degradations are path-specific between client01/client02 and rnp_rj, characterized by elevated RTT and throughput variability during 2024-05-07→06-01 (client01), 2024-07-11→07-18 (client01), and 2024-05-08→06-15 (client02). Investigate routing/congestion on these paths.
- Actions:
  - Run time-correlated traceroute/MTR from client01 and client02 to rnp_rj (and reverse) during similar dayparts to those windows to detect path changes or hop-specific latency.
  - Check BGP/IGP change logs and interface counters (errors/drops/queueing) on the rnp_rj ingress/egress facing client01/client02 networks around the cited dates.
  - If available, compare queue/traffic shaping policies applied to client01/client02 paths versus other clients that remained stable.

====================================================================================================
[RTT_UPLOAD] STAGE 4 – RESPONSE
====================================================================================================
## QoS Assessment Report: rtt_upload Approach

### 1) Executive Summary
The rtt_upload-based analysis separates intervals into two distinct operational states. Cluster 0 exhibits a high-risk, unstable profile with short-lived intervals, frequent change events, higher RTTs, and lower/more variable throughput. Cluster 1 is low-risk and stable, with sustained low RTT and high throughput. Logistic regression identifies client09, client12, client02, client07, and server rnp_rj as most associated with the high-risk Cluster 0. Diagnoses show a recurrent, path-specific impairment toward site gru03 affecting multiple clients (client09, client12, client07), a client/server-path instability between client01/client02 and rnp_rj during May–June, and a late-window onset of instability to rnp_sp for client07.

### 2) Detailed Findings

- Cluster risk profiles (rtt_upload approach)
  - Cluster 0 (high risk): Short intervals (mean ~10.55, median ~4.39), high event_frequency (0.645), survival decay from 0.91 (day 1) to 0.11 (day 60/90). Higher RTTs (rtt_d ~96.35 ms; rtt_u ~98.06 ms) and lower, more variable throughput (down ~306.6 Mbps; up ~281.0 Mbps).
  - Cluster 1 (low risk): Long intervals (mean ~31.45, median ~28.98), very low event_frequency (0.030), strong survival (≥0.933 through day 60/90). Low RTTs (rtt_d ~17.95 ms; rtt_u ~20.41 ms) and higher, more stable throughput (down ~660.7 Mbps; up ~660.0 Mbps).

- Critical elements (logistic regression association to Cluster 0; negative coefficients decrease odds of Cluster 1)
  - client09 (coef −0.9923): strongest client-side tendency toward Cluster 0.
  - rnp_rj (coef −0.9031): strongest server-side association to Cluster 0.
  - client12 (coef −0.7821), client02 (coef −0.6515), client07 (coef −0.6036): additional strong associations to Cluster 0.

- Interval diagnoses for critical elements
  - client09:
    - Stable/low-risk (Cluster 1) to gru05, gru06, rnp_rj, rnp_sp with excellent throughput (down 831–905 Mbps; up 859–871 Mbps) and low RTT (12–20 ms).
    - High-risk exclusively to gru03: most of May–June at ~170/182 Mbps with RTT ~116–121 ms; 2024-06-28→07-01 severe dip (down 93.9 Mbps; up 100.4 Mbps; RTT ~212 ms); 2024-07-09→07-19 RTT normalizes (~15 ms) and upload recovers (~867 Mbps) but download remains ~172 Mbps; 2024-07-19→07-23 download unstable (mean ~147 Mbps; very high std) despite low RTT and high upload. Cluster 0 intervals heavily event-flagged.
    - Interpretation: path/server-specific issue toward gru03; post-07-09 routing/latency improved, but downstream (server-to-client) throughput remains unstable.

  - rnp_rj (server):
    - 44 intervals: 33 Cluster 1 (~75%), 11 Cluster 0 (~25%). All Cluster 0 are event-flagged.
    - client01: Stable low RTT baseline outside two degradation windows:
      - 2024-05-07→06-01: rtt_d ~18.5–21.5 ms (up from ~7 ms), variable rtt_u; indication of elevated latency.
      - 2024-07-11→07-18: fluctuating RTT and download mean drop to ~495 Mbps (std ~72 Mbps).
    - client02: 2024-05-08→06-15 prolonged Cluster 0 with uplink RTT elevation/variance (rtt_u up to ~25–26 ms) and download dips/variance (down ~554 Mbps, std ~78 Mbps), then recovery.
    - Other clients (03–16): entirely Cluster 1 with rnp_rj.
    - Interpretation: degradations are path-specific to client01/client02 toward rnp_rj; no server-wide instability detected.

  - client12:
    - Spent 9/17 intervals in Cluster 1 and 8/17 in Cluster 0.
    - gru03: 2024-05-06→07-09 persistent high-risk with severe RTT inflation (up to ~223–229 ms) and throughput collapse (down ~81 Mbps during 06-28→07-01). 07-01→07-09 remains unstable (RTT ~75–105 ms). After 2024-07-09, flips to Cluster 1 with strong throughput (~670–748 Mbps) and low RTT (~14–19 ms).
    - gru05: Mostly stable, brief Cluster 0 2024-07-08→07-14 driven by higher/variable download RTT (~26.7 ms); throughput largely unchanged.
    - rnp_sp: Short Cluster 0 2024-07-07→07-17 with elevated upload RTT (~14.33 ms vs ~11.68 prior), then recovery.
    - rnp_rj and gru06: Stable Cluster 1 throughout.
    - Interpretation: long-standing path issue to gru03 resolved around 2024-07-09; minor transient RTT increases to gru05/rnp_sp in mid-July.

  - client02:
    - Mostly stable (Cluster 1 = 7/12) across gru03, gru05, gru06; typical metrics down ~582–593 Mbps, up ~296–301 Mbps; RTT down ~9–12 ms (SP/GRU), ~16–17 ms (RJ).
    - High-risk only with RNP paths:
      - rnp_sp: 2024-05-06→05-08 modest RTT increase (rtt_d ~11.07 ms) and higher variability; then stable.
      - rnp_rj: 2024-05-08→06-15 multiple Cluster 0 intervals; worst 2024-06-02→06-04 with down ~554 Mbps (std ~78 Mbps) and rtt_u ~25.4 ms with high variance; lower download persisted until 06-15; recovered thereafter with rtt_u still moderately higher than early May.
    - Interpretation: RJ path/server-specific instability in May–June; no issues to GRU sites.

  - client07:
    - Mixed stability: 60% Cluster 1, 40% Cluster 0.
    - gru03: All intervals in Cluster 0. 2024-06-28→07-01 severe (down/up ~95/104 Mbps; RTT ~207/212 ms). 07-09→07-23 RTT improves (~20 ms) and throughput means rise (~538/619 Mbps) but with very high variability; still classified as Cluster 0.
    - rnp_sp: Stable until 2024-07-16, then Cluster 0 (07-16→07-23) with highly variable downstream throughput (mean ~522 Mbps, std ~421 Mbps) while RTT stays low.
    - gru05, gru06, rnp_rj: Stable Cluster 1, high throughput (~760–860 Mbps down; ~550–790 Mbps up) and low RTT (~12–20 ms).
    - Interpretation: persistent path impairment to gru03 with partial recovery but ongoing instability; new throughput instability to rnp_sp starting mid-July with low RTT.

- Cross-cutting observations
  - Recurrent impairment to gru03 across client09, client12, and client07 with severe RTT inflation and throughput collapse around 2024-06-28→07-01, followed by post-2024-07-09 RTT normalization but lingering downstream throughput instability in some cases.
  - Path-specific degradations to rnp_rj confined to client01 and client02 during May–June; other clients remained stable, indicating no server-wide issue.
  - A late-window, throughput-only instability to rnp_sp emerges for client07 (07-16→07-23) with RTT remaining low.

### 3) Recommended Actions

- Path validation and routing analysis
  - Run traceroute/MTR in both directions for:
    - client09/client12/client07 ↔ gru03 (peak and off-peak), focusing on pre- vs post-2024-07-09 paths.
    - client01/client02 ↔ rnp_rj during comparable dayparts to the May–June degradation windows.
    - client07 ↔ rnp_sp, comparing pre- (≤07-15) and post-onset (≥07-16) paths.
  - Review BGP/IGP change logs and route policies affecting GRU03, RNP-RJ, and RNP-SP around:
    - 2024-06-28→07-01 and 2024-07-09 (gru03),
    - 2024-05-08→06-15 (rnp_rj),
    - 2024-07-16 (rnp_sp).

- Server and interface telemetry
  - Check gru03 server and adjacent interfaces: CPU/NIC utilization, queue occupancy, drops, AQM behavior, TCP retransmissions, especially 06-28→07-01 and immediately after 07-09.
  - Inspect rnp_rj ingress/egress facing client01/client02 networks for errors/drops/queueing during 05-08→06-15; corroborate with traffic/load on the server.
  - Audit rnp_sp for throughput-plane anomalies starting 07-16 (NIC/queue saturation, shaping changes).

- Directional performance testing
  - Run parallel iperf3/NDT from affected clients to:
    - gru03 vs a healthy control (e.g., gru06) to isolate download-only degradation.
    - rnp_rj and rnp_sp vs controls, during peak and off-peak.
  - Augment with active loss/latency probes (e.g., OWAMP/TWAMP) to detect microbursts or intermittent loss where RTT remains low but throughput is unstable.

- Configuration and policy review
  - Verify any mid-July adjustments that could explain RTT normalization to gru03 but residual downstream instability (QoS/shaping, asymmetric routing, ECN/AQM configs).
  - Compare queue/traffic shaping policies applied to client01/client02 paths versus other clients that remained stable to rnp_rj.

- Monitoring and escalation
  - Set targeted alerts for:
    - RTT_u spikes >2× baseline and download throughput variance surges to gru03 and rnp_rj.
    - Sudden increases in downstream throughput variance to rnp_sp.
  - If path asymmetry or upstream congestion is confirmed, engage peering partners and RNP operations with the time-bound evidence above.

==== Throughput approach ====
## QoS Assessment Report: throughput_download Approach

### 1) Executive Summary
Two distinct performance clusters emerged. Cluster 1 represents a low-risk, stable profile (longer intervals, low event frequency, higher download/upload throughput, and low RTT). Cluster 0 represents a high-risk, unstable profile (shorter intervals, high event frequency, lower throughput, and elevated RTT). Logistic regression associates client02, client07, rnp_rj, and rnp_sp with higher odds of Cluster 0. Time-series diagnoses show: (a) client07 shifted to unstable download performance from mid-June toward rnp_rj and rnp_sp without RTT inflation, and had earlier high-RTT issues to gru03 that later normalized; (b) client02 is predominantly Cluster 0, with notable download variability toward gru05; (c) rnp_rj and rnp_sp show coordinated mid-July download collapses across multiple clients while uploads and RTT largely remained stable, consistent with downstream capacity/policy effects near the servers or adjacent peering. Cluster labels align with aggregated risk, but are not a perfect proxy per client/site, so interval metrics and timing remain essential.

### 2) Detailed Findings

- Cluster risk profiles (aggregated)
  - Cluster 1 (low-risk, stable): Long intervals (≈24.29 days), very low event frequency (0.078), strong survival (≥0.887 at 30 days), higher throughput (DL ≈632.5, UL ≈684.3), and low RTT (DL ≈16.0 ms, UL ≈18.9 ms).
  - Cluster 0 (high-risk, unstable): Short intervals (≈10.64 days), high event frequency (0.738), fast decay (survival 0.553 at 7 days; ~0.18 at 15–30 days), lower throughput (DL ≈452.1, UL ≈429.3), and elevated RTT (DL ≈59.6 ms, UL ≈60.6 ms).

- Critical elements (logistic regression; higher odds of Cluster 0)
  - Clients: client02 (coef −1.95), client07 (coef −1.73).
  - Servers: rnp_rj (coef −1.37), rnp_sp (coef −1.23).

- Interval diagnoses

  client02
  - Cluster distribution: 24/28 intervals (86%) in Cluster 0; 4/28 (14%) in Cluster 1.
  - RNP paths (rnp_sp, rnp_rj): Mostly steady behavior across May–Jul with two short degradations:
    - rnp_rj: 2024-06-02 to 2024-06-04, DL dipped to ~554 Mbps with high variability (std ~78 Mbps); RTT_upload_mean ~25.38 ms.
    - rnp_sp: 2024-06-06 to 2024-06-07, DL ~579.7 Mbps with elevated variability (std ~37 Mbps); recovered afterward.
  - GRU paths (gru03, gru05, gru06): Present throughout; gru05 shows a long sub-interval 2024-05-11 to 2024-07-15 with lower DL (~557 Mbps) and very high variability (std ~92.6 Mbps), indicating sustained instability on that path.
  - Note: Although Cluster 1 is generally stable in aggregate, for client02 the GRU-assigned intervals include the most pronounced download variability (gru05), underscoring that cluster labels are not a perfect per-path proxy.

  client07
  - Cluster distribution: 17/27 (≈63%) in Cluster 0; 10/27 (≈37%) in Cluster 1.
  - Mid-June shift to instability toward RNP servers:
    - rnp_sp: from 2024-06-12, multiple change-point intervals move to Cluster 0; by 2024-07-06–07-23 DL mean ≈522 Mbps with extremely high variance (std ≈421 Mbps); RTT stable (~13–15 ms).
    - rnp_rj: from 2024-06-16, Cluster 0 intervals accumulate; 2024-07-06–07-22 DL ≈376–380 Mbps with very high std (≈304–405 Mbps); RTT ~18–20 ms and stable.
  - gru03 path: 2024-05-02 to 2024-07-09 had abnormally high RTT (~120–212 ms) with modest DL; 2024-07-09–07-17 transient high DL/low RTT but high variance; after 2024-07-17, stabilized with low RTT (~14–20 ms).
  - Consistently healthy to GRU: gru05 and gru06 stayed in Cluster 1 with high throughput and low RTT.

  rnp_rj
  - Cluster mix: 42/76 (≈55%) in Cluster 1; 34/76 (≈45%) in Cluster 0.
  - Coordinated mid-July download degradations (uploads and RTT roughly steady at ~18–24 ms):
    - 2024-07-17→07-23 affecting multiple clients: client08 (DL ~182.6 Mbps), client09 (~219.2 Mbps), client13 (~248.6 Mbps), client14 (~220.4 Mbps), client16 (~182.3 Mbps), and client07 (longer low-DL spell ~376–380 Mbps, 07-06→07-22).
  - Notable routing/latency change: client04 from 2024-06-26 shows RTT_upload ≈30.4 ms (up from ~2–3 ms) while maintaining high throughput—consistent with a path change rather than capacity loss.

  rnp_sp
  - Cluster mix: 34/58 (≈58.6%) in Cluster 1; 24/58 (≈41.4%) in Cluster 0.
  - Mid-July download ceiling across multiple clients with stable upload:
    - 2024-07-17→07-23: client08 (DL ~175.7 Mbps; RTT_download ~26.4 ms with high jitter), client12 (~232.6→~219.0 Mbps), client13 (~174→~177 Mbps), client14 (~176.5 Mbps), client16 (~176.3 Mbps). Upload sustained high (~790–830 Mbps); RTT mostly near historical values except client08.
  - Precursors:
    - client07: 2024-07-06→07-23, DL mean ≈522 Mbps with very high variance (std ≈421 Mbps), RTT stable.
    - client09: 2024-07-04→07-09, DL instability (mean ≈629 Mbps, std ≈387 Mbps); partial recovery with lingering variance.
  - Cluster label caveat: Some Cluster 0 intervals reflect high performance (e.g., client04 ~910.6 Mbps DL on 2024-06-26→07-01), so label ≠ universal “bad.”

### 3) Recommended Actions

- Correlate and reproduce mid-July degradations
  - Run parallel NDT/iperf tests during comparable hours from affected clients (07, 08, 09, 12, 13, 14, 16) to rnp_rj and rnp_sp, plus an unaffected control path (e.g., client02→RNP).
  - Capture TCP retransmissions/loss, cwnd evolution, and per-flow goodput; compare download vs upload asymmetries.

  - Perform bidirectional traceroute/MTR during degradation windows; record AS paths/hop stability and per-hop loss/latency.
  - Review BGP/peering change logs and link utilizations around 2024-07-17→07-23 (rnp_rj and rnp_sp) and 2024-06-12→06-16 (client07 onset).

- Server-side and adjacent interface checks (rnp_rj, rnp_sp)
  - Inspect egress/ingress interface counters (drops, discards), queue/AQM stats, shaping/policing policies, and any recent config changes.
  - Specifically validate absence of download-path rate limits near ~200 Mbps on/near rnp_sp in 2024-07-17→07-23.

- Client-specific investigations
  - client07: Audit access/aggregation device counters for drops/oversubscription; verify no new policers/shapers introduced mid-June. Repeat sustained tests to rnp_rj/rnp_sp and confirm stable RTT with signs of loss-based throughput collapse.
  - client02: Target gru05/gru03/gru06 vs rnp_sp as control; correlate high download variability (2024-05-11→07-15 to gru05) with path/routing/interface data; check for intermittent congestion on GRU segments.

- Historic anomalies
  - Validate routing changes for client04→rnp_rj around 2024-06-26 that raised RTT without throughput loss.
  - For client07→gru03, confirm route normalization post 2024-07-09 with baseline RTT monitoring.

- Monitoring and alerting
  - Implement detectors for synchronized multi-client download depressions and for “download ceiling” patterns (e.g., ~200 Mbps) with unaffected upload/RTT.
  - Track cluster event rates per client/server to catch rising instability before customer impact.

## Final Task

Your final task is to write a single, consolidated Operational Report for the network operations and field teams. This report MUST be written in clear, direct Brazilian Portuguese (PT-BR).

Structure the report as follows:

### 1. Executive Summary: Capacity vs. Responsiveness

Explain in simple terms why we analyze the network from these two perspectives (one measures “delivery capacity” and the other the “responsiveness of the connection”) and what the main finding was for each.

### 2. Unified Diagnosis: Where Are the Critical Points?

  - Action 1 (NOC): Immediately check the server’s monitoring dashboards for hardware alerts (CPU, memory) and link saturation.
  - Action 2 (Field Team): Run a `traceroute` from a problematic client (e.g., `client12`) to `gru03` to map the path and identify hops with high latency.
  - Action 3 (Engineering): Escalate to the Network Engineering team to review routing policies and the peering agreement with RNP at this location.

Be objective and focus on verifiable actions. Avoid statistical or machine learning jargon.
